Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Yeah, um… I guess I came in because I’ve just felt kind of weighed down for a long time. Like this flat, heavy feeling that’s just there most days. Even when work is fine, or people are being decent to me, it doesn’t really lift. I still feel down, kind of empty.

I mean, I almost didn’t come in because I kept thinking maybe I was just tired or being dramatic, and then I’d get distracted by work stuff—spreadsheets, meetings, all that—but the feeling is still there when I’m done. So… yeah, that’s what brought me in.

Yeah… I do, I think. Maybe not always “guilty” in a dramatic way, but I blame myself a lot.

Like at work, people still bring up this dashboard I built, and they’ll say it was helpful, and instead of just taking that in, my first thought is kind of like… they don’t realize I just got lucky, or I somehow made myself look more competent than I am.

And then if I miss a follow-up or send an email late, even something small, it feels like evidence that I’m not actually good at this. Which sounds kind of stupid when I say it out loud. I don’t know. I’ll also start thinking about how I should have worded something differently, and then I’ll open my inbox to check, and then somehow I’m looking at the formatting of a slide deck from three weeks ago… sorry, I’m getting off track.

But yeah, I do blame myself for things more than I probably should.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I do. Probably more than I’d like to admit.

Like at work, people still bring up this dashboard I built, and they’ll say it was helpful or whatever, but my first thought is usually that I just somehow made it look better than it was, or I kind of fooled them into thinking I knew what I was doing. And then if I miss a follow-up or send an email late, it feels like, there it is, that’s the proof that I’m not actually as capable as they think.

Which is stupid, because everyone misses emails sometimes, right? But it doesn’t really feel like that in my head. It feels like evidence.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, um… I guess I came in because I’ve just felt kind of weighed down for a long time. Like this flat, heavy feeling that’s just there most days. Even when work is fine, or people are being decent to me, it doesn’t really lift. I still feel down, kind of empty.\n\nI mean, I almost didn’t come in because I kept thinking maybe I was just tired or being dramatic, and then I’d get distracted by work stuff—spreadsheets, meetings, all that—but the feeling is still there when I’m done. So… yeah, that’s what brought me in.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been a little over two years, I think. Like, about two years and two months.\n\nI remember it starting around when this dashboard project at work kind of put me in the spotlight, which sounds stupid because it was technically a good thing. People were paying attention to what I was doing, asking me for updates, and I had all these tabs and charts open all the time… Sorry, that’s not really the point. But yeah, ever since around then, it’s just been there.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like, or has that changed since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. Like… I think I still do things I used to like, but whether I enjoy them the same way is hard to tell.\n\nSometimes I’ll put on a show or scroll around or meet someone for coffee, and it’s fine, I guess. But then I start thinking about something unrelated, like whether I replied to an email or if I forgot to update a spreadsheet, and I kind of lose track of whether I was enjoying it in the first place. So… maybe it’s changed? I don’t know. It’s not super clear to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “lost interest,” exactly. Like, I still do some of the same things, or I’ll plan to do them, and then I get kind of sidetracked in my head.\n\nIt’s weird because if someone asks, “Do you like this?” I’m like, yeah, probably. But then I’ll be sitting there watching something or doing whatever, and I’m thinking about whether I have clean shirts for the week or if I left a tab open on my work laptop. So I’m not sure if that means I’m not interested anymore or if I’m just… not really present for it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? I mean… I eat, but I don’t really track it closely. Some days I’ll get busy and realize I haven’t had lunch, but that also used to happen when I had back-to-back stuff at work, so I don’t know if that’s a change.\n\nI did buy this meal prep container set thinking it would make me more organized, and then I only used, like, two of them because they’re annoying to wash… sorry, that’s not really about appetite. I guess I haven’t noticed anything major.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I sleep, but I don’t know if it’s different in a way I’d call a pattern.\n\nSometimes I’ll stay up later than I meant to because I’m looking at something dumb on my phone, and then I’ll start reorganizing tabs or checking tomorrow’s calendar, which is probably not helpful. But I also feel like I’ve always done that a little? Like in school I used to do the same thing before assignments were due.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t noticed a clear change. I’d have to think about it more.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Especially by the afternoon. I’ll be at work and something small comes up, like there’s an error in a spreadsheet or I need to finish expense paperwork, and it feels way harder than it should.\n\nIt’s not like I’m running a marathon or anything, but it’s like I’m dragging my body and my brain through mud. Even getting myself to soccer after work can feel like this whole production, and I used to just… go. Now I’ll sit there thinking about my cleats or whether I need to bring a water bottle, and somehow that feels exhausting too.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, I think. Maybe not always “guilty” in a dramatic way, but I blame myself a lot.\n\nLike at work, people still bring up this dashboard I built, and they’ll say it was helpful, and instead of just taking that in, my first thought is kind of like… they don’t realize I just got lucky, or I somehow made myself look more competent than I am.\n\nAnd then if I miss a follow-up or send an email late, even something small, it feels like evidence that I’m not actually good at this. Which sounds kind of stupid when I say it out loud. I don’t know. I’ll also start thinking about how I should have worded something differently, and then I’ll open my inbox to check, and then somehow I’m looking at the formatting of a slide deck from three weeks ago… sorry, I’m getting off track.\n\nBut yeah, I do blame myself for things more than I probably should.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts about death or dying?\n\nPatient: I… don’t know. I still feel down, kind of empty.\n\nI mean, I almost didn’t come in because I kept thinking maybe I was just tired or being dramatic, and then I’d get distracted by work stuff—spreadsheets, meetings, all that—but the feeling is still there when I’m done. So… yeah, that’s what brought me in.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been a little over two years, I think. Like, about two years and two months.\n\nI remember it starting around when this dashboard project at work kind of put me in the spotlight, which sounds stupid because it was technically a good thing. People were paying attention to what I was doing, asking me for updates, and I had all these tabs and charts open all the time… Sorry, that’s not really the point. But yeah, ever since around then, it’s just been there.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like, or has that changed since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. Like… I think I still do things I used to like, but whether I enjoy them the same way is hard to tell.\n\nSometimes I’ll put on a show or scroll around or meet someone for coffee, and it’s fine, I guess. But then I start thinking about something unrelated, like whether I replied to an email or if I forgot to update a spreadsheet, and I kind of lose track of whether I was enjoying it in the first place. So… maybe it’s changed? I don’t know. It’s not super clear to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “lost interest,” exactly. Like, I still do some of the same things, or I’ll plan to do them, and then I get kind of sidetracked in my head.\n\nIt’s weird because if someone asks, “Do you like this?” I’m like, yeah, probably. But then I’ll be sitting there watching something or doing whatever, and I’m thinking about whether I have clean shirts for the week or if I left a tab open on my work laptop. So I’m not sure if that means I’m not interested anymore or if I’m just… not really present for it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? I mean… I eat, but I don’t really track it closely. Some days I’ll get busy and realize I haven’t had lunch, but that also used to happen when I had back-to-back stuff at work, so I don’t know if that’s a change.\n\nI did buy this meal prep container set thinking it would make me more organized, and then I only used, like, two of them because they’re annoying to wash… sorry, that’s not really about appetite. I guess I haven’t noticed anything major.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I sleep, but I don’t know if it’s different in a way I’d call a pattern.\n\nSometimes I’ll stay up later than I meant to because I’m looking at something dumb on my phone, and then I’ll start reorganizing tabs or checking tomorrow’s calendar, which is probably not helpful. But I also feel like I’ve always done that a little? Like in school I used to do the same thing before assignments were due.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t noticed a clear change. I’d have to think about it more.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Especially by the afternoon. I’ll be at work and something small comes up, like there’s an error in a spreadsheet or I need to finish expense paperwork, and it feels way harder than it should.\n\nIt’s not like I’m running a marathon or anything, but it’s like I’m dragging my body and my brain through mud. Which sounds kind of stupid when I say it out loud. I don’t know. I’ll also start thinking about how I should have worded something differently, and then I’ll open my inbox to check, and then somehow I’m looking at the formatting of a slide deck from three weeks ago… sorry, I’m getting off track.\n\nBut yeah, I do blame myself for things more than I probably should.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts about death or dying?\n\nPatient: I… don’t know. Not in a way I’ve really sat down and labeled, I guess.\n\nSometimes when I’m really worn out I’ll think about how weird it is that everyone just has to keep doing all this stuff every day, like emails and laundry and pretending to know what you’re doing at work. I’m… not sure I’d put it that way. Like, I don’t think I’ve had that exact thought of “I’d be better off dead.”

Sometimes I get into this loop where I feel like I’m not really doing much right, and then I’ll start thinking about work stuff or some random thing I forgot to do, like renewing my parking permit or whatever, and it all kind of piles up. But that’s more like feeling overwhelmed or disappointed in myself, not… wanting to be dead.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, um… I guess I came in because I’ve just felt kind of weighed down for a long time. Like this flat, heavy feeling that’s just there most days. Even when work is fine, or people are being decent to me, it doesn’t really lift. I still feel down, kind of empty.\n\nI mean, I almost didn’t come in because I kept thinking maybe I was just tired or being dramatic, and then I’d get distracted by work stuff—spreadsheets, meetings, all that—but the feeling is still there when I’m done. So… yeah, that’s what brought me in.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been a little over two years, I think. Like, about two years and two months.\n\nI remember it starting around when this dashboard project at work kind of put me in the spotlight, which sounds stupid because it was technically a good thing. People were paying attention to what I was doing, asking me for updates, and I had all these tabs and charts open all the time… Sorry, that’s not really the point. But yeah, ever since around then, it’s just been there.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like, or has that changed since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. Like… I think I still do things I used to like, but whether I enjoy them the same way is hard to tell.\n\nSometimes I’ll put on a show or scroll around or meet someone for coffee, and it’s fine, I guess. But then I start thinking about something unrelated, like whether I replied to an email or if I forgot to update a spreadsheet, and I kind of lose track of whether I was enjoying it in the first place. So… maybe it’s changed? I don’t know. It’s not super clear to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “lost interest,” exactly. Like, I still do some of the same things, or I’ll plan to do them, and then I get kind of sidetracked in my head.\n\nIt’s weird because if someone asks, “Do you like this?” I’m like, yeah, probably. But then I’ll be sitting there watching something or doing whatever, and I’m thinking about whether I have clean shirts for the week or if I left a tab open on my work laptop. So I’m not sure if that means I’m not interested anymore or if I’m just… not really present for it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? I mean… I eat, but I don’t really track it closely. Some days I’ll get busy and realize I haven’t had lunch, but that also used to happen when I had back-to-back stuff at work, so I don’t know if that’s a change.\n\nI did buy this meal prep container set thinking it would make me more organized, and then I only used, like, two of them because they’re annoying to wash… sorry, that’s not really about appetite. I guess I haven’t noticed anything major.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I sleep, but I don’t know if it’s different in a way I’d call a pattern.\n\nSometimes I’ll stay up later than I meant to because I’m looking at something dumb on my phone, and then I’ll start reorganizing tabs or checking tomorrow’s calendar, which is probably not helpful. But I also feel like I’ve always done that a little? Like in school I used to do the same thing before assignments were due.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t noticed a clear change. I’d have to think about it more.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Especially by the afternoon. I’ll be at work and something small comes up, like there’s an error in a spreadsheet or I need to finish expense paperwork, and it feels way harder than it should.\n\nIt’s not like I’m running a marathon or anything, but it’s like I’m dragging my body and my brain through mud. Even getting myself to soccer after work can feel like this whole production, and I used to just… go. Now I’ll sit there thinking about my cleats or whether I need to bring a water bottle, and somehow that feels exhausting too.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, I think. Maybe not always “guilty” in a dramatic way, but I blame myself a lot.\n\nLike at work, people still bring up this dashboard I built, and they’ll say it was helpful, and instead of just taking that in, my first thought is kind of like… they don’t realize I just got lucky, or I somehow made myself look more competent than I am.\n\nAnd then if I miss a follow-up or send an email late, even something small, it feels like evidence that I’m not actually good at this. Which sounds kind of stupid when I say it out loud. I don’t know. I’ll also start thinking about how I should have worded something differently, and then I’ll open my inbox to check, and then somehow I’m looking at the formatting of a slide deck from three weeks ago… sorry, I’m getting off track.\n\nBut yeah, I do blame myself for things more than I probably should.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts about death or dying?\n\nPatient: I… don’t know. Not in a way I’ve really sat down and labeled, I guess.\n\nSometimes when I’m really worn out I’ll think about how weird it is that everyone just has to keep doing all this stuff every day, like emails and laundry and pretending to know what you’re doing at work. But then my brain kind of wanders off to something else, like I’ll remember I forgot to pay a bill or I need to text someone back.\n\nSo… I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. What do you mean by thoughts about death?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that you would be better off dead or that dying might be a way to escape your problems?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure. I don’t think I’ve had it that clearly, like “I’d be better off dead.” That sounds pretty intense.\n\nSometimes I’ll think more like… it would be nice if everything just stopped for a while, if that makes sense? Like not work, not messages, not having to keep track of all the little things. But then I get caught up thinking about some random thing I need to do, like whether I replied to a Teams message or if I left laundry in the washer, and I don’t really stay on that thought.\n\nSo I guess… I don’t know if that counts. I don’t think of it as wanting to die exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself repeatedly thinking about death, even if it's not about wanting to die?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not repeatedly, I mean. I don’t really sit around thinking about death as a topic.\n\nSometimes something random will set me off for a second, like seeing some headline or thinking about how fast time goes, and then I’ll get this weird heavy feeling. But then I’m usually distracted by something else, like checking my calendar or remembering I didn’t answer an email, or I’ll start thinking about what I’m supposed to make for dinner and it just kind of… moves on.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think it’s a repeated thing. Or at least I haven’t noticed it that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself criticizing yourself or doubting your abilities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. Probably more than I’d like to admit.\n\nLike at work, people still bring up this dashboard I built, and they’ll say it was helpful or whatever, but my first thought is usually that I just somehow made it look better than it was, or I kind of fooled them into thinking I knew what I was doing. But it doesn’t really feel like that in my head. It feels like evidence.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think I’ve noticed anything significant. But then I’m usually distracted by something else, like checking my calendar or remembering I didn’t answer an email, or I’ll start thinking about what I’m supposed to make for dinner and it just kind of… moves on.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think it’s a repeated thing. Or at least I haven’t noticed it that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself criticizing yourself or doubting your abilities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. Probably more than I’d like to admit.\n\nLike at work, people still bring up this dashboard I built, and they’ll say it was helpful or whatever, but my first thought is usually that I just somehow made it look better than it was, or I kind of fooled them into thinking I knew what I was doing. And then if I miss a follow-up or send an email late, it feels like, there it is, that’s the proof that I’m not actually as capable as they think.\n\nWhich is stupid, because everyone misses emails sometimes, right? But it doesn’t really feel like that in my head. It feels like evidence.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think I’ve noticed anything significant. Sometimes I’ll notice jeans feel tighter after I’ve been eating takeout for a few days, but that’s more like… normal fluctuation, I guess. And then I get distracted trying to remember if I bought groceries or if there’s just condiments in the fridge, so I don’t really track it.\n\nSo… no major weight change that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that you would be better off dead?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I’d put it that way. Like, I don’t think I’ve had that exact thought of “I’d be better off dead.”\n\nSometimes I get into this loop where I feel like I’m not really doing much right, and then I’ll start thinking about work stuff or some random thing I forgot to do, like renewing my parking permit or whatever, and it all kind of piles up. Most of the day, I think. It’s not like it hits for ten minutes and then I’m fine.

I mean, I sleep, but I don’t know if it’s different in a way I’d call a pattern.\n\nSometimes I’ll stay up later than I meant to because I’m looking at something dumb on my phone, and then I’ll start reorganizing tabs or checking tomorrow’s calendar, which is probably not helpful. But I also feel like I’ve always done that a little? Like in school I used to do the same thing before assignments were due.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t noticed a clear change. I’d have to think about it more.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Especially by the afternoon. I’ll be at work and something small comes up, like there’s an error in a spreadsheet or I need to finish expense paperwork, and it feels way harder than it should.\n\nIt’s not like I’m running a marathon or anything, but it’s like I’m dragging my body and my brain through mud. Even getting myself to soccer after work can feel like this whole production, and I used to just… go. Now I’ll sit there thinking about my cleats or whether I need to bring a water bottle, and somehow that feels exhausting too.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, I think. Maybe not always “guilty” in a dramatic way, but I blame myself a lot.\n\nLike at work, people still bring up this dashboard I built, and they’ll say it was helpful, and instead of just taking that in, my first thought is kind of like… they don’t realize I just got lucky, or I somehow made myself look more competent than I am.\n\nAnd then if I miss a follow-up or send an email late, even something small, it feels like evidence that I’m not actually good at this. Which sounds kind of stupid when I say it out loud. I don’t know. I’ll also start thinking about how I should have worded something differently, and then I’ll open my inbox to check, and then somehow I’m looking at the formatting of a slide deck from three weeks ago… sorry, I’m getting off track.\n\nBut yeah, I do blame myself for things more than I probably should.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts about death or dying?\n\nPatient: I… don’t know. Not in a way I’ve really sat down and labeled, I guess.\n\nSometimes when I’m really worn out I’ll think about how weird it is that everyone just has to keep doing all this stuff every day, like emails and laundry and pretending to know what you’re doing at work. But then my brain kind of wanders off to something else, like I’ll remember I forgot to pay a bill or I need to text someone back.\n\nSo… I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. What do you mean by thoughts about death?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that you would be better off dead or that dying might be a way to escape your problems?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure. I don’t think I’ve had it that clearly, like “I’d be better off dead.” That sounds pretty intense.\n\nSometimes I’ll think more like… it would be nice if everything just stopped for a while, if that makes sense? Like not work, not messages, not having to keep track of all the little things. But then I get caught up thinking about some random thing I need to do, like whether I replied to a Teams message or if I left laundry in the washer, and I don’t really stay on that thought.\n\nSo I guess… I don’t know if that counts. I don’t think of it as wanting to die exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself repeatedly thinking about death, even if it's not about wanting to die?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not repeatedly, I mean. I don’t really sit around thinking about death as a topic.\n\nSometimes something random will set me off for a second, like seeing some headline or thinking about how fast time goes, and then I’ll get this weird heavy feeling. But then I’m usually distracted by something else, like checking my calendar or remembering I didn’t answer an email, or I’ll start thinking about what I’m supposed to make for dinner and it just kind of… moves on.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think it’s a repeated thing. Or at least I haven’t noticed it that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself criticizing yourself or doubting your abilities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. Probably more than I’d like to admit.\n\nLike at work, people still bring up this dashboard I built, and they’ll say it was helpful or whatever, but my first thought is usually that I just somehow made it look better than it was, or I kind of fooled them into thinking I knew what I was doing. And then if I miss a follow-up or send an email late, it feels like, there it is, that’s the proof that I’m not actually as capable as they think.\n\nWhich is stupid, because everyone misses emails sometimes, right? But it doesn’t really feel like that in my head. It feels like evidence.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think I’ve noticed anything significant. I don’t really weigh myself regularly, though, so I’m kind of guessing.\n\nMy clothes seem mostly the same, I think. Sometimes I’ll notice jeans feel tighter after I’ve been eating takeout for a few days, but that’s more like… normal fluctuation, I guess. And then I get distracted trying to remember if I bought groceries or if there’s just condiments in the fridge, so I don’t really track it.\n\nSo… no major weight change that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that you would be better off dead?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I’d put it that way. Like, I don’t think I’ve had that exact thought of “I’d be better off dead.”\n\nSometimes I get into this loop where I feel like I’m not really doing much right, and then I’ll start thinking about work stuff or some random thing I forgot to do, like renewing my parking permit or whatever, and it all kind of piles up. But that’s more like feeling overwhelmed or disappointed in myself, not… wanting to be dead.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think so. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Are your feelings of being weighed down or empty usually present for most of the day, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: Most of the day, I think. It’s not like it hits for ten minutes and then I’m fine.\n\nIt’s more like… this flat, heavy thing I’m carrying around. Even if work is going okay, or someone is nice to me, I’ll still feel kind of down or empty underneath it. Like the situation might be normal, but my mood doesn’t really rise to meet it, if that makes sense.\n\nI mean, I can get distracted by stuff — emails, errands, random things — but the feeling is usually still there most days.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Now I’ll sit there thinking about my cleats or whether I need to bring a water bottle, and somehow that feels exhausting too.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, I think. Maybe not always “guilty” in a dramatic way, but I blame myself a lot.\n\nLike at work, people still bring up this dashboard I built, and they’ll say it was helpful, and instead of just taking that in, my first thought is kind of like… they don’t realize I just got lucky, or I somehow made myself look more competent than I am.\n\nAnd then if I miss a follow-up or send an email late, even something small, it feels like evidence that I’m not actually good at this. Which sounds kind of stupid when I say it out loud. I don’t know. I’ll also start thinking about how I should have worded something differently, and then I’ll open my inbox to check, and then somehow I’m looking at the formatting of a slide deck from three weeks ago… sorry, I’m getting off track.\n\nBut yeah, I do blame myself for things more than I probably should.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts about death or dying?\n\nPatient: I… don’t know. Not in a way I’ve really sat down and labeled, I guess.\n\nSometimes when I’m really worn out I’ll think about how weird it is that everyone just has to keep doing all this stuff every day, like emails and laundry and pretending to know what you’re doing at work. But then my brain kind of wanders off to something else, like I’ll remember I forgot to pay a bill or I need to text someone back.\n\nSo… I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. What do you mean by thoughts about death?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that you would be better off dead or that dying might be a way to escape your problems?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure. I don’t think I’ve had it that clearly, like “I’d be better off dead.” That sounds pretty intense.\n\nSometimes I’ll think more like… it would be nice if everything just stopped for a while, if that makes sense? Like not work, not messages, not having to keep track of all the little things. But then I get caught up thinking about some random thing I need to do, like whether I replied to a Teams message or if I left laundry in the washer, and I don’t really stay on that thought.\n\nSo I guess… I don’t know if that counts. I don’t think of it as wanting to die exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself repeatedly thinking about death, even if it's not about wanting to die?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not repeatedly, I mean. I don’t really sit around thinking about death as a topic.\n\nSometimes something random will set me off for a second, like seeing some headline or thinking about how fast time goes, and then I’ll get this weird heavy feeling. But then I’m usually distracted by something else, like checking my calendar or remembering I didn’t answer an email, or I’ll start thinking about what I’m supposed to make for dinner and it just kind of… moves on.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think it’s a repeated thing. Or at least I haven’t noticed it that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself criticizing yourself or doubting your abilities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. Probably more than I’d like to admit.\n\nLike at work, people still bring up this dashboard I built, and they’ll say it was helpful or whatever, but my first thought is usually that I just somehow made it look better than it was, or I kind of fooled them into thinking I knew what I was doing. And then if I miss a follow-up or send an email late, it feels like, there it is, that’s the proof that I’m not actually as capable as they think.\n\nWhich is stupid, because everyone misses emails sometimes, right? But it doesn’t really feel like that in my head. It feels like evidence.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think I’ve noticed anything significant. I don’t really weigh myself regularly, though, so I’m kind of guessing.\n\nMy clothes seem mostly the same, I think. Sometimes I’ll notice jeans feel tighter after I’ve been eating takeout for a few days, but that’s more like… normal fluctuation, I guess. And then I get distracted trying to remember if I bought groceries or if there’s just condiments in the fridge, so I don’t really track it.\n\nSo… no major weight change that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that you would be better off dead?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I’d put it that way. Like, I don’t think I’ve had that exact thought of “I’d be better off dead.”\n\nSometimes I get into this loop where I feel like I’m not really doing much right, and then I’ll start thinking about work stuff or some random thing I forgot to do, like renewing my parking permit or whatever, and it all kind of piles up. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Are your feelings of being weighed down or empty usually present for most of the day, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: Most of the day, I think. It’s not like it hits for ten minutes and then I’m fine.\n\nIt’s more like… this flat, heavy thing I’m carrying around. Even if work is going okay, or someone is nice to me, I’ll still feel kind of down or empty underneath it. Like the situation might be normal, but my mood doesn’t really rise to meet it, if that makes sense.\n\nI mean, I can get distracted by stuff — emails, errands, random things — but the feeling is usually still there most days.\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling of being weighed down or empty been continuous for the entire two years and two months, or have you had periods where it lifted or got better?\n\nPatient: It’s been… pretty much the whole time, yeah. About two years and two months, I guess, since that dashboard project at work sort of put me in the spotlight.\n\nI mean, there are little stretches where I’m distracted or I’m busy with something, and I’ll think, okay, maybe I’m doing better. Like if there’s a meeting that goes smoothly, or I spend a whole afternoon fixing some spreadsheet thing and I don’t have to think too much. But then it’s still kind of there underneath.\n\nThat project was when people started noticing my work more, which sounds like it should’ve been good, but it just… I don’t know, it felt like everything got heavier after that. So I wouldn’t say it really lifted in any lasting way.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods, even brief ones, where you felt completely free of this weighed-down feeling since it started two years and two months ago?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s been a time where it was totally gone, no. It’s more like… maybe I’m busy enough that I’m not paying attention to it for a bit.\n\nLike, since that dashboard project at work — the one that kind of put me in the spotlight — it’s been about two years and two months of this. I don’t think so.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you had any periods, even brief ones, where you felt significantly better than you do now since this started two years and two months ago?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 22 / management_analyst / some_college\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Duration\nmanifestation: \"This has been going on for about two years and two months, ever since the dashboard project put me in the spotlight at work.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean… maybe a little better here and there, but not really in a way that felt like it was over.

It’s been going on about two years and two months now, since that dashboard project at work put me in the spotlight. I keep coming back to that because it’s like the marker in my head for when things shifted. Which is strange, because everyone else treated it like a good thing — and I guess it was — but after that, I just felt heavier.

There might’ve been days where work was smoother or I was busy and didn’t notice it as much, but I don’t know if I’d call that significantly better. More like… temporarily distracted.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n18\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F33.0, F33.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Yeah, um… I guess I came in because I’ve just felt kind of weighed down for a long time. Like this flat, heavy feeling that’s just there most days. Even when work is fine, or people are being decent to me, it doesn’t really lift. I still feel down, kind of empty.\n\nI mean, I almost didn’t come in because I kept thinking maybe I was just tired or being dramatic, and then I’d get distracted by work stuff—spreadsheets, meetings, all that—but the feeling is still there when I’m done. So… yeah, that’s what brought me in.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… it’s been a little over two years, I think. Like, about two years and two months.\n\nI remember it starting around when this dashboard project at work kind of put me in the spotlight, which sounds stupid because it was technically a good thing. People were paying attention to what I was doing, asking me for updates, and I had all these tabs and charts open all the time… Sorry, that’s not really the point. But yeah, ever since around then, it’s just been there.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like, or has that changed since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. Like… I think I still do things I used to like, but whether I enjoy them the same way is hard to tell.\n\nSometimes I’ll put on a show or scroll around or meet someone for coffee, and it’s fine, I guess. But then I start thinking about something unrelated, like whether I replied to an email or if I forgot to update a spreadsheet, and I kind of lose track of whether I was enjoying it in the first place. So… maybe it’s changed? I don’t know. It’s not super clear to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “lost interest,” exactly. Like, I still do some of the same things, or I’ll plan to do them, and then I get kind of sidetracked in my head.\n\nIt’s weird because if someone asks, “Do you like this?” I’m like, yeah, probably. But then I’ll be sitting there watching something or doing whatever, and I’m thinking about whether I have clean shirts for the week or if I left a tab open on my work laptop. So I’m not sure if that means I’m not interested anymore or if I’m just… not really present for it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your appetite since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so? I mean… I eat, but I don’t really track it closely. Some days I’ll get busy and realize I haven’t had lunch, but that also used to happen when I had back-to-back stuff at work, so I don’t know if that’s a change.\n\nI did buy this meal prep container set thinking it would make me more organized, and then I only used, like, two of them because they’re annoying to wash… sorry, that’s not really about appetite. I guess I haven’t noticed anything major.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I sleep, but I don’t know if it’s different in a way I’d call a pattern.\n\nSometimes I’ll stay up later than I meant to because I’m looking at something dumb on my phone, and then I’ll start reorganizing tabs or checking tomorrow’s calendar, which is probably not helpful. But I also feel like I’ve always done that a little? Like in school I used to do the same thing before assignments were due.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t noticed a clear change. I’d have to think about it more.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Especially by the afternoon. I’ll be at work and something small comes up, like there’s an error in a spreadsheet or I need to finish expense paperwork, and it feels way harder than it should.\n\nIt’s not like I’m running a marathon or anything, but it’s like I’m dragging my body and my brain through mud. Even getting myself to soccer after work can feel like this whole production, and I used to just… go. Now I’ll sit there thinking about my cleats or whether I need to bring a water bottle, and somehow that feels exhausting too.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, I think. Maybe not always “guilty” in a dramatic way, but I blame myself a lot.\n\nLike at work, people still bring up this dashboard I built, and they’ll say it was helpful, and instead of just taking that in, my first thought is kind of like… they don’t realize I just got lucky, or I somehow made myself look more competent than I am.\n\nAnd then if I miss a follow-up or send an email late, even something small, it feels like evidence that I’m not actually good at this. Which sounds kind of stupid when I say it out loud. I don’t know. I’ll also start thinking about how I should have worded something differently, and then I’ll open my inbox to check, and then somehow I’m looking at the formatting of a slide deck from three weeks ago… sorry, I’m getting off track.\n\nBut yeah, I do blame myself for things more than I probably should.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts about death or dying?\n\nPatient: I… don’t know. Not in a way I’ve really sat down and labeled, I guess.\n\nSometimes when I’m really worn out I’ll think about how weird it is that everyone just has to keep doing all this stuff every day, like emails and laundry and pretending to know what you’re doing at work. But then my brain kind of wanders off to something else, like I’ll remember I forgot to pay a bill or I need to text someone back.\n\nSo… I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. What do you mean by thoughts about death?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that you would be better off dead or that dying might be a way to escape your problems?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure. I don’t think I’ve had it that clearly, like “I’d be better off dead.” That sounds pretty intense.\n\nSometimes I’ll think more like… it would be nice if everything just stopped for a while, if that makes sense? Like not work, not messages, not having to keep track of all the little things. But then I get caught up thinking about some random thing I need to do, like whether I replied to a Teams message or if I left laundry in the washer, and I don’t really stay on that thought.\n\nSo I guess… I don’t know if that counts. I don’t think of it as wanting to die exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself repeatedly thinking about death, even if it's not about wanting to die?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not repeatedly, I mean. I don’t really sit around thinking about death as a topic.\n\nSometimes something random will set me off for a second, like seeing some headline or thinking about how fast time goes, and then I’ll get this weird heavy feeling. But then I’m usually distracted by something else, like checking my calendar or remembering I didn’t answer an email, or I’ll start thinking about what I’m supposed to make for dinner and it just kind of… moves on.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think it’s a repeated thing. Or at least I haven’t noticed it that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself criticizing yourself or doubting your abilities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. Probably more than I’d like to admit.\n\nLike at work, people still bring up this dashboard I built, and they’ll say it was helpful or whatever, but my first thought is usually that I just somehow made it look better than it was, or I kind of fooled them into thinking I knew what I was doing. And then if I miss a follow-up or send an email late, it feels like, there it is, that’s the proof that I’m not actually as capable as they think.\n\nWhich is stupid, because everyone misses emails sometimes, right? But it doesn’t really feel like that in my head. It feels like evidence.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think I’ve noticed anything significant. I don’t really weigh myself regularly, though, so I’m kind of guessing.\n\nMy clothes seem mostly the same, I think. Sometimes I’ll notice jeans feel tighter after I’ve been eating takeout for a few days, but that’s more like… normal fluctuation, I guess. And then I get distracted trying to remember if I bought groceries or if there’s just condiments in the fridge, so I don’t really track it.\n\nSo… no major weight change that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that you would be better off dead?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I’d put it that way. Like, I don’t think I’ve had that exact thought of “I’d be better off dead.”\n\nSometimes I get into this loop where I feel like I’m not really doing much right, and then I’ll start thinking about work stuff or some random thing I forgot to do, like renewing my parking permit or whatever, and it all kind of piles up. But that’s more like feeling overwhelmed or disappointed in myself, not… wanting to be dead.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think so. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Are your feelings of being weighed down or empty usually present for most of the day, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: Most of the day, I think. It’s not like it hits for ten minutes and then I’m fine.\n\nIt’s more like… this flat, heavy thing I’m carrying around. Even if work is going okay, or someone is nice to me, I’ll still feel kind of down or empty underneath it. Like the situation might be normal, but my mood doesn’t really rise to meet it, if that makes sense.\n\nI mean, I can get distracted by stuff — emails, errands, random things — but the feeling is usually still there most days.\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling of being weighed down or empty been continuous for the entire two years and two months, or have you had periods where it lifted or got better?\n\nPatient: It’s been… pretty much the whole time, yeah. About two years and two months, I guess, since that dashboard project at work sort of put me in the spotlight.\n\nI mean, there are little stretches where I’m distracted or I’m busy with something, and I’ll think, okay, maybe I’m doing better. Like if there’s a meeting that goes smoothly, or I spend a whole afternoon fixing some spreadsheet thing and I don’t have to think too much. But then it’s still kind of there underneath.\n\nThat project was when people started noticing my work more, which sounds like it should’ve been good, but it just… I don’t know, it felt like everything got heavier after that. So I wouldn’t say it really lifted in any lasting way.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods, even brief ones, where you felt completely free of this weighed-down feeling since it started two years and two months ago?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s been a time where it was totally gone, no. It’s more like… maybe I’m busy enough that I’m not paying attention to it for a bit.\n\nLike, since that dashboard project at work — the one that kind of put me in the spotlight — it’s been about two years and two months of this. Which is weird because people acted like it was a good thing, and I guess it was, career-wise, but after that I just felt like I was being watched more or something. I don’t think so.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods, even brief ones, where you felt significantly better than you do now since this started two years and two months ago?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe a little better here and there, but not really in a way that felt like it was over.\n\nIt’s been going on about two years and two months now, since that dashboard project at work put me in the spotlight. I guess I haven’t noticed anything major.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I sleep, but I don’t know if it’s different in a way I’d call a pattern.\n\nSometimes I’ll stay up later than I meant to because I’m looking at something dumb on my phone, and then I’ll start reorganizing tabs or checking tomorrow’s calendar, which is probably not helpful. But I also feel like I’ve always done that a little? Like in school I used to do the same thing before assignments were due.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t noticed a clear change. I’d have to think about it more.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel tired or lacking in energy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Especially by the afternoon. I’ll be at work and something small comes up, like there’s an error in a spreadsheet or I need to finish expense paperwork, and it feels way harder than it should.\n\nIt’s not like I’m running a marathon or anything, but it’s like I’m dragging my body and my brain through mud. Even getting myself to soccer after work can feel like this whole production, and I used to just… go. Now I’ll sit there thinking about my cleats or whether I need to bring a water bottle, and somehow that feels exhausting too.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do, I think. Maybe not always “guilty” in a dramatic way, but I blame myself a lot.\n\nLike at work, people still bring up this dashboard I built, and they’ll say it was helpful, and instead of just taking that in, my first thought is kind of like… they don’t realize I just got lucky, or I somehow made myself look more competent than I am.\n\nAnd then if I miss a follow-up or send an email late, even something small, it feels like evidence that I’m not actually good at this. Which sounds kind of stupid when I say it out loud. I don’t know. I’ll also start thinking about how I should have worded something differently, and then I’ll open my inbox to check, and then somehow I’m looking at the formatting of a slide deck from three weeks ago… sorry, I’m getting off track.\n\nBut yeah, I do blame myself for things more than I probably should.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts about death or dying?\n\nPatient: I… don’t know. Not in a way I’ve really sat down and labeled, I guess.\n\nSometimes when I’m really worn out I’ll think about how weird it is that everyone just has to keep doing all this stuff every day, like emails and laundry and pretending to know what you’re doing at work. But then my brain kind of wanders off to something else, like I’ll remember I forgot to pay a bill or I need to text someone back.\n\nSo… I’m not sure how to answer that exactly. What do you mean by thoughts about death?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that you would be better off dead or that dying might be a way to escape your problems?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure. I don’t think I’ve had it that clearly, like “I’d be better off dead.” That sounds pretty intense.\n\nSometimes I’ll think more like… it would be nice if everything just stopped for a while, if that makes sense? Like not work, not messages, not having to keep track of all the little things. But then I get caught up thinking about some random thing I need to do, like whether I replied to a Teams message or if I left laundry in the washer, and I don’t really stay on that thought.\n\nSo I guess… I don’t know if that counts. I don’t think of it as wanting to die exactly.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever found yourself repeatedly thinking about death, even if it's not about wanting to die?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not repeatedly, I mean. I don’t really sit around thinking about death as a topic.\n\nSometimes something random will set me off for a second, like seeing some headline or thinking about how fast time goes, and then I’ll get this weird heavy feeling. But then I’m usually distracted by something else, like checking my calendar or remembering I didn’t answer an email, or I’ll start thinking about what I’m supposed to make for dinner and it just kind of… moves on.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think it’s a repeated thing. Or at least I haven’t noticed it that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself criticizing yourself or doubting your abilities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. Probably more than I’d like to admit.\n\nLike at work, people still bring up this dashboard I built, and they’ll say it was helpful or whatever, but my first thought is usually that I just somehow made it look better than it was, or I kind of fooled them into thinking I knew what I was doing. And then if I miss a follow-up or send an email late, it feels like, there it is, that’s the proof that I’m not actually as capable as they think.\n\nWhich is stupid, because everyone misses emails sometimes, right? But it doesn’t really feel like that in my head. It feels like evidence.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think I’ve noticed anything significant. I don’t really weigh myself regularly, though, so I’m kind of guessing.\n\nMy clothes seem mostly the same, I think. Sometimes I’ll notice jeans feel tighter after I’ve been eating takeout for a few days, but that’s more like… normal fluctuation, I guess. And then I get distracted trying to remember if I bought groceries or if there’s just condiments in the fridge, so I don’t really track it.\n\nSo… no major weight change that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that you would be better off dead?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I’d put it that way. Like, I don’t think I’ve had that exact thought of “I’d be better off dead.”\n\nSometimes I get into this loop where I feel like I’m not really doing much right, and then I’ll start thinking about work stuff or some random thing I forgot to do, like renewing my parking permit or whatever, and it all kind of piles up. But that’s more like feeling overwhelmed or disappointed in myself, not… wanting to be dead.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think so. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Are your feelings of being weighed down or empty usually present for most of the day, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: Most of the day, I think. It’s not like it hits for ten minutes and then I’m fine.\n\nIt’s more like… this flat, heavy thing I’m carrying around. Even if work is going okay, or someone is nice to me, I’ll still feel kind of down or empty underneath it. Like the situation might be normal, but my mood doesn’t really rise to meet it, if that makes sense.\n\nI mean, I can get distracted by stuff — emails, errands, random things — but the feeling is usually still there most days.\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling of being weighed down or empty been continuous for the entire two years and two months, or have you had periods where it lifted or got better?\n\nPatient: It’s been… pretty much the whole time, yeah. About two years and two months, I guess, since that dashboard project at work sort of put me in the spotlight.\n\nI mean, there are little stretches where I’m distracted or I’m busy with something, and I’ll think, okay, maybe I’m doing better. Like if there’s a meeting that goes smoothly, or I spend a whole afternoon fixing some spreadsheet thing and I don’t have to think too much. But then it’s still kind of there underneath.\n\nThat project was when people started noticing my work more, which sounds like it should’ve been good, but it just… I don’t know, it felt like everything got heavier after that. So I wouldn’t say it really lifted in any lasting way.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods, even brief ones, where you felt completely free of this weighed-down feeling since it started two years and two months ago?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s been a time where it was totally gone, no. It’s more like… maybe I’m busy enough that I’m not paying attention to it for a bit.\n\nLike, since that dashboard project at work — the one that kind of put me in the spotlight — it’s been about two years and two months of this. Which is weird because people acted like it was a good thing, and I guess it was, career-wise, but after that I just felt like I was being watched more or something. Not watched, exactly, but… you know.\n\nSo, yeah, I’ve had moments where I’m distracted, but completely free of it? I don’t think so.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods, even brief ones, where you felt significantly better than you do now since this started two years and two months ago?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe a little better here and there, but not really in a way that felt like it was over.\n\nIt’s been going on about two years and two months now, since that dashboard project at work put me in the spotlight. I keep coming back to that because it’s like the marker in my head for when things shifted. More like… temporarily distracted.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods, even brief ones, where you felt completely back to your normal self since this started two years and two months ago?\n\nPatient: Completely back to normal? That was when people started paying more attention to me, which, I mean, I know sounds like a good thing. Or at least I haven’t noticed it that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself criticizing yourself or doubting your abilities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. Probably more than I’d like to admit.\n\nLike at work, people still bring up this dashboard I built, and they’ll say it was helpful or whatever, but my first thought is usually that I just somehow made it look better than it was, or I kind of fooled them into thinking I knew what I was doing. And then if I miss a follow-up or send an email late, it feels like, there it is, that’s the proof that I’m not actually as capable as they think.\n\nWhich is stupid, because everyone misses emails sometimes, right? But it doesn’t really feel like that in my head. It feels like evidence.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant change in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think I’ve noticed anything significant. I don’t really weigh myself regularly, though, so I’m kind of guessing.\n\nMy clothes seem mostly the same, I think. Sometimes I’ll notice jeans feel tighter after I’ve been eating takeout for a few days, but that’s more like… normal fluctuation, I guess. And then I get distracted trying to remember if I bought groceries or if there’s just condiments in the fridge, so I don’t really track it.\n\nSo… no major weight change that I’m aware of.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts that you would be better off dead?\n\nPatient: I’m… not sure I’d put it that way. Like, I don’t think I’ve had that exact thought of “I’d be better off dead.”\n\nSometimes I get into this loop where I feel like I’m not really doing much right, and then I’ll start thinking about work stuff or some random thing I forgot to do, like renewing my parking permit or whatever, and it all kind of piles up. But that’s more like feeling overwhelmed or disappointed in myself, not… wanting to be dead.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think so. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Are your feelings of being weighed down or empty usually present for most of the day, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: Most of the day, I think. It’s not like it hits for ten minutes and then I’m fine.\n\nIt’s more like… this flat, heavy thing I’m carrying around. Even if work is going okay, or someone is nice to me, I’ll still feel kind of down or empty underneath it. Like the situation might be normal, but my mood doesn’t really rise to meet it, if that makes sense.\n\nI mean, I can get distracted by stuff — emails, errands, random things — but the feeling is usually still there most days.\n\nDoctor: Has this feeling of being weighed down or empty been continuous for the entire two years and two months, or have you had periods where it lifted or got better?\n\nPatient: It’s been… pretty much the whole time, yeah. About two years and two months, I guess, since that dashboard project at work sort of put me in the spotlight.\n\nI mean, there are little stretches where I’m distracted or I’m busy with something, and I’ll think, okay, maybe I’m doing better. Like if there’s a meeting that goes smoothly, or I spend a whole afternoon fixing some spreadsheet thing and I don’t have to think too much. But then it’s still kind of there underneath.\n\nThat project was when people started noticing my work more, which sounds like it should’ve been good, but it just… I don’t know, it felt like everything got heavier after that. So I wouldn’t say it really lifted in any lasting way.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods, even brief ones, where you felt completely free of this weighed-down feeling since it started two years and two months ago?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there’s been a time where it was totally gone, no. Which is weird because people acted like it was a good thing, and I guess it was, career-wise, but after that I just felt like I was being watched more or something. Not watched, exactly, but… you know.\n\nSo, yeah, I’ve had moments where I’m distracted, but completely free of it? I don’t think so.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods, even brief ones, where you felt significantly better than you do now since this started two years and two months ago?\n\nPatient: I mean… maybe a little better here and there, but not really in a way that felt like it was over.\n\nIt’s been going on about two years and two months now, since that dashboard project at work put me in the spotlight. I keep coming back to that because it’s like the marker in my head for when things shifted. Which is strange, because everyone else treated it like a good thing — and I guess it was — but after that, I just felt heavier.\n\nThere might’ve been days where work was smoother or I was busy and didn’t notice it as much, but I don’t know if I’d call that significantly better.
